# VOLUME 09 BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 0411 TO 0420, AT `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, AND NOTHING ABOVE THIS POINT IS REWRITTEN

*Appended downward by the phase that took the second chapter batch of Volume 09, after the first chapter batch, after the repair that took `logs/batch-0001.review.log`, after the volume outline and the repair that read it. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no canon fact, no date, no room, no distance, no name, no figure and no calendar is changed by this section, except in one place where this batch refused a premise in its own prompt and the refusal is entered here with the wrong claim named and deleted nowhere.** This batch wrote ten files into `chapters/volume-09/` and opened no chapter of any closed volume.*

**The calendar this batch ran on, with the derivation before the date and the finding in it.** Chapter 0410 is the second day of the second week of the fourth month of the year after the year after the year after the year after the year after next, and it is the last date the previous batch fixed. **One seven-day step from it is the second day of the third week of the fourth month, and Chapter 0411 is in the fifth month, and that is only possible if the fourth month of the seventh named year holds two weeks, which it does, being Chapters 0409 and 0410, and a month of two weeks is a finding and not a defect.** A month in this calendar is four weeks and the day inside a week is the inherited cycle. **The two numbers a spine is checkable with, both read out of the ten date lines: the day inside the week, which holds second, second, fourth, fourth, restarting with each month, with no exception in ten files; and the number of weeks in the month, which is two in the fourth, four in the fifth, four in the sixth, and two so far in the seventh.** The ten date lines read: second first fifth, second second fifth, fourth third fifth, fourth fourth fifth, second first sixth, second second sixth, fourth third sixth, fourth fourth sixth, second first seventh, second second seventh. **All ten files carry a date line, which is the second batch running in which that is true.** The blind spot: **the sixth named year was found to be two weeks short because two of its months were three weeks long, and a clean first movement is not a clean year, and the seventh named year has now produced a two-week month in its second, which is a shorter month than the shortest one on the page, and it is recorded here and not smoothed over by moving a week.**

**And the premise this batch refused, which is a first in this project and is the reason this paragraph is the first one in this section.** The prompt's section Five states the ten dates for this batch and states them as the fourth day of the first week of the fifth month, the fourth day of the second week of the fifth month, the second day of the third week of the fifth month, the second day of the fourth week of the fifth month, and so on, month by month. **Those ten are the exact inversion of the inherited cycle, week for week, in all ten entries, and the wrong claim is named here and deleted nowhere.** The cycle was held for three reasons, and the reason is not deference to a record. *One:* the cycle is a standing lock that has held without exception across all fifty files of Volume 08 and all ten files of Batch 0001, and this project's own recorded finding is that the **day** inside a week is the number that has never broken and the **number of weeks in a month** is the number that has. *Two:* the prompt's list does not close against the derivation the prompt itself states, and a seven-day step from the second day of the second week of the fourth month is never the fourth day of the first week of the fifth month, on any length of the fourth month. *Three:* a month is permitted to be short by the project's own standing and a day is not, and **a premise handed to a phase is not a fact, and a phase that cannot reproduce one says so and does not restate it.** Nothing was repaired and no week was chosen, and the two-week month is a consequence of holding the cycle and it is carried forward and not hidden.

**The protagonist's chapters, derived by reading the ten files and not by a string, and the two sweeps beside them with the standing.** Three chapters and in person in two of the three, the want visible in two: **0413, 0417 and 0420**, and the sentence that puts him in each is printed in this file, in the batch summary and in the character state, because a count is not an answer to a floor. 0413 — *"I have known the price of it since a man came up those four flights on the fourth day of the third week of the third month, and Marek Kest is the name on him, and he did not buy a copy of anything and went back down them, and it is six weeks since and I have had all six of them to work out what he came for."* 0417 — *"He is thirty-eight and his name is Marek Kest, and he is not at a stage and is not going to arrive at one."* 0420 — *"He has a name, Marek Kest, and he is not a lawyer, and there is no office behind him of any kind and no money, no list, no leave and no certification, and none of those five is on its way."* **`grep -l 'Kest'` returns three and is right. `grep -l 'thirty-eight'` returns two, 0417 and 0420, and both of them are him. The union of the two sweeps is three and the answer is three, and that is the first time in two volumes that the union has been right, and the standing is unchanged by it: the floor is counted by reading and never by a string, and the reason the sweep agreed this time is a sentence and not a virtue — no other person in these ten files is thirty-eight, so the age sweep had nothing to be wrong about. The last batch's age sweep returned a woman buying fourpence worth of paper and the one before that returned a book-keeper twice, and a check that comes back with the right answer is not a check that can see.** His want is visible in 0417 and in 0420 and is printed in no chapter, and the name of the hand that struck the line out of the leaf is printed in no chapter, and his reason for not reaching a stage is referred to nowhere in these ten files and paid a second time zero times. He accepted nothing: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage, no certification, no reading, no exercise of the thing he is able to do, no second copy of anything, and **he is not a carrier and 0417 is the chapter in which the cheapest party in this matter is told his price out loud and does not become findable.**

**The question marks, and the definition beside them.** `grep -c '?'` over the ten files returns **zero in all ten.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven, it is seven at Chapter 0350, seven at Chapter 0400, seven when this batch was written, and seven now, and **the number is printed in none of the ten chapters and the count is named once, in the volume's own formula, in 0420, and it is not the closing line there.** The blind spot is the standing from the last batch and it is a craft tool and not a defect: **a count of question marks is blind by construction to a question-shaped sentence that carries no mark, and a count of askings is blind to it too, so the sentence does the work of a question in a chapter where nothing may be asked.** And the method that found this batch's askings was the same one the last batch's repair used: **five of them, at 0411, 0413, 0415, 0416 and 0420, found by reading the batch for the class and not by running anything against it, and all five are statements now.** A class is a better instrument than an instance, and the instance is what a reading reports, and neither class is visible to any check this project owns, because every check here reads a string and an asking is a thing a person did.

**The paper.** A carrier is a person holding a thing that is not theirs, that has not been signed for, and that cannot be refused, and it is said out loud in five rooms in these ten chapters and in the same words once. **The volume's smallest object did not move: no blank was filled in, the box under the far end of those boards was not opened and did not move, the drawer was not opened, no line was ruled, no ruled space under a printed heading was filled, no fifth column was ruled in a day-book that has stood on a shelf behind a counter for about nine years with four headings over it, and the day-book was not read and was not turned round, and the volume's smallest completed act — a man putting one on a table — is Movement Five's and did not happen.** **About four hundred blanks a year go out of that counter in the hand of somebody and the case they come out of is still not the box, and the case is named in three of these ten chapters and the box in four, and the clerk of about twenty-three is a person and not a column: a woman of about thirty and a woman of about twenty-nine each buy one at fourpence in this batch and are asked nothing and say nothing.** The word *blank* is printed at the lock end in 0416 **once**, because 0408, which is also a chapter about the lock, has already said in this volume what a blank is, and the two chapters do not say it in the same words.

**The things that were named and the things that were not, entered so a later reader does not read this section as a plot change.** Nobody was thanked, forgiven or sent for. Nothing is resolved and the arrangement is still standing. The second half of the question is not restated and the decision about whether Tamsin Rook trusts a colleague's judgment is not made in 0418 and is not made anywhere. The who is not named and the name of the hand that struck the line is not named. The next asking is not made anybody's and the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand is not asked anything. The figure at the end of the cold passage does not get a heading and is not given away in any form, in any sentence, in any shape. The question in the second of the eleven books does not get a full stop and is not printed out in any of the ten, and its author is not asked a second time. The two people in their boxes stay in them and the form that put them there is not asked to be void, and the two of seventeen at the back of a shed are not named in any of the ten. The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen are not spoken to, unthanked and unsent for, and the girl is not given the chance to say no. The two sealed pages stay shut with two refusals between them and no third. The three stitches stay three things. The counting-house mark stays a mark. The four appearances of the substituted wording keep their four ages and this batch adds no fifth. **The four hundred and thirty miles, the nine miles and the four hundred yards of cold flags are each walked zero times in these ten chapters, and the four hundred and thirty is named in 0417 as a road that is not going to be walked on his account, which is the page's own arrangement and not a journey.** The bill at Lowcross is unpaid, nobody is liable, and **it is named zero times in the ten chapters, which is eight batches in a row and is a decision and not an oversight, and the batch summary names it and no figure.** **The second hand's own figures are not used at all in these ten chapters — about nine hundred sheets a year and about nine of them a year that are not what the person above him wrote — and he is asked nothing, and the nine miles is not walked, and the four men in that bay are not asked one question about a number.** Tamsin Rook's undertaking is live, unretired and unexercised and its date is not said out loud in any of the ten and is not said out loud by this state record either.

**The three corrections in the state layer, propagated into every row written this batch and closed as nothing, because they are not plot threads.** **The woman of about forty with a scuttle is in fourteen of the four hundred closed files and Chapter 0362 is named for her and is set in the room with the rail from its first paragraph to its last — the false claim that she is in none of Volume 08's fifty files is named in this batch's records and deleted nowhere — and she is in no chapter of this batch, and 0416 is a chapter at the other end of the same four hundred yards and does not put her in it, and a Volume 09 writer may not give her a room, a trade, a week or a history the page has not given her.** **The woman of about twenty-six is at the lock and the room with the rail is at the other end of the same four hundred yards and is not hers and is entered twice a week by the woman of about forty — the false placement put her in another woman's room, which is a merge this project has spent a volume preventing, and 0416 is written from the lock end, enters nothing else and walks the passage zero times.** **`outline/volume-09.md` exists and is 111,429 bytes across 265 lines and is titled *The Blank* and is the outline of record for Chapters 0401 to 0450 — the false claim that no outline of record exists stood in this project's state layer in six places, is named beside the right one in every row this batch wrote, and is deleted nowhere, and this batch's ten cards are Movement Two of that outline's five movements and its declarations section is what stopped a writer from inheriting a coalition, a convention, a charter, a mountain and a coup from the series plan.**

**And the two further premises in this batch's own prompt that did not hold the page, entered here because the standing is that a writer who is handed a false premise is the only person in the chain who can catch it, and both are named and neither is deleted.** *One, the gender of the man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner.* The prompt's card for 0415 calls him **the young woman** and the prompt's card for 0419 calls him **the man**, and `outline/volume-09.md` says *a man of about twenty-six paid by the corner* in two places and the state layer says it, and **closed Chapter 0396 says *The young man who carries the sheets away* in its eleventh line and *The young woman took his hand off the sill. He was twenty-six.* in its thirty-first, and 0396 is closed and no phase may open it.** The page and the outline of record are with the two, so both chapters are written with a man, and the disagreement inside 0396 is carried unrepaired on purpose, because a repair that picked would be a fourth figure where two are already unreconciled, which is the same standing that carries item 83. *Two, the age of the fourth item on the fee board.* The prompt's card for 0420 has the clerk of about twenty-nine say the fourth item **has been on it eleven years for the same reason the box has been under those boards eleven years**, and **Chapter 0353 says in terms that Marn Ottery wrote that item herself in her own hand about four years ago, and a fourth-year item and an eleventh-year box are two objects and not one.** The clerk's argument in 0420 is therefore carried by the **shape** and not by the figure, and she says the two figures out loud in the wrong order on purpose, and the reader is given both numbers and can check them. **The prompt's eleven years is not carried into any chapter.**

**The figures, with the method before each, taken after the last edit this phase made to any chapter.** Ten word counts by `sed 's/[[:space:]]*$//' file | wc -w`, one file at a time and never with a glob: **1,638 / 1,369 / 1,781 / 1,546 / 1,453 / 1,323 / 1,529 / 1,547 / 1,615 / 1,577**, the batch at **15,378**, the manuscript at 420 files and **1,121,580**, the four hundred closed files at **1,091,877**, and 1,121,580 less 14,325 less 15,378 is 1,091,877 exactly, which is the check that says this phase opened no closed chapter. **The concatenations of the manuscript and of the closed four hundred return 1,121,577 and 1,091,874, three words fewer each, and the three words are three Volume 05 files that do not end in a newline, and both figures are printed because a reader holding one of them needs to know which object it is, and the ten new files all end in a newline so the ten's two readings are identical at 15,378.** The nine volumes, summed per file, stand at 202,117 / 184,039 / 176,097 / 148,821 / 144,248 / 101,261 / 72,149 / 63,145 / 29,703. The blind spot: a word count cannot see whether the words are the same words.

**The paragraph sweeps, classified before they are reported clean, which is the standing and which this batch exercised three times.** Tokenised on whitespace, the leading heading line stripped and then not stripped, against all four hundred closed files. **External shared twenty-word runs: 13, and all 13 are formula — ten date lines, two windows out of the one canon sentence the man of about sixty-one says to a pen pot in 0415, and one dated week inside a mouth in 0419. Prose: zero. Internal shared twenty-word prose runs across the ten, date lines removed: zero.** **The third of the thirteen was classified harmless by this record and by the reading of this batch, and both of them were wrong to leave it there unchecked: the dated week is how Chapter 0419 dates a disclosure to closed Chapter 0396, the day, the week and the month in it were right, the year name in it was the year after 0396's and is now 0396's, and the sweep detected the run and could not see the error because the run it matches begins at the day and a twenty-word window that starts at *the fourth day* cannot reach the year name. A check that returns a run is not a check that has read the run, and this record printed the classification and not the sentence, which is the eighth time this project has let a classification stand in for a reading.** **The fifteen prose runs the first pass found are named in the batch summary and were all in files this phase had written minutes earlier, and the biggest of them were the second hand's corridor, his own introduction, the four going up and being signed for, and the sentence he says to the pot, and the woman of twenty-six and the cold and the rack, and the day-book woman and the two knocks, and the leaf in 0420 against the leaf in 0417.** **The blind spot of this check is the finding of this batch and it belongs here: a window asks whether two files agree, and a canon line said by the person it belongs to agrees with the closed chapter it came from, so the sweep is measuring the sentence and not the theft, and it reported 0415's sentence as shared material and 0415 is a man saying his own line in his own corridor for the third time in his life.** Measured directly at 256 words, the ten files return **zero** against the closed page, against the inherited bound of 255. The twelve-word test returns 428 and is **not** reported clean for anything: it is blind to a re-printed identification sentence that has been reworded and it is blind to a canon line in its owner's mouth, and 428 against the last batch's 485 is a fall and not a pass.

**The sentence bound, by a named splitter, run on the ten and on Volume 08's fifty with the same code, and the tool's blindness named beside its result.** The ten at **572 sentences, mean 26.2, median 20, p90 59, maximum 85, 8.0 per cent over sixty words and 19.2 per cent over forty-five.** Volume 08's fifty at **2,241 sentences, mean 27.5, median 24, p90 55, maximum 88, 5.4 per cent over sixty and 19.6 per cent over forty-five.** **Five sentences in this batch were over eighty-eight words when they were first measured — a hundred and fifteen, a hundred and thirteen, a hundred and eight, a hundred and three and ninety-nine — and all five were broken by hand in the last edit, and the maximum is now eighty-five, which is under the closed page's eighty-eight, and none of the five was a sentence anybody wanted whole.** The blind spot: a sentence bound is a reading of a tool and not of the page, and this splitter joins a dialogue block across a newline where a speaker's turn has no terminal punctuation, so the figure is printed with the tool named.

**The remaining sweeps.** `Lowcross` returns zero files and zero occurrences across the ten, which is eight batches in a row. **`this year` returns zero, and it does, and that is a change from the last batch, which returned one at 0407:29, and the change is not luck: the two places this batch drafted the phrase were caught by reading before the file was closed.** The superseded volume title and the plan's nouns return zero. **No calendar month name and no weekday name is in any of the ten, and the one false positive a verifier gets is the modal verb *may*, once, in 0420.** One hundred bold spans across the ten after the repair that took `logs/batch-0002.review.log`, and it was ninety-eight, no duplicated marker pair and no unclosed marker, no `***`, one heading a file. **One hundred and seventy-two uses of *about* after the repair, and it was one hundred and sixty-nine, measured before and after with the one method this file carries, `\babout\b` per file and summed, at 23 / 14 / 15 / 16 / 13 / 10 / 23 / 18 / 23 / 14 and at 25 / 14 / 15 / 17 / 13 / 10 / 23 / 18 / 23 / 14:** the prepositional uses are ordinary English and are not hedges, and the hedges that remain are the page's own figures.

**The four debts, restated in full at the foot of this section, and this phase discharges none of them.** An owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three. The review gate, which has opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, **and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either.** And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, which are now **eighty-nine by the high-water mark, eighty-seven entries measured in the rolling ledger before the eighth enforcement and three in it now, and the sixty-three this project has been quoting is twenty-four short, which is item 90, and this batch neither corrected that figure in place nor repeated it as true.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, it reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned`, and it is carried and not repaired. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and a batch is not among them, and a batch that inherited a ledger and no debt has been misinformed by somebody. The third of those debts has an eighth instance now, and the number of instances is not a measure of the debt: this batch wrote ten chapters into the second movement of the first volume that has an outline of record, and nobody but the hand that wrote them has read them, and this file is not a review and does not report itself as one.**

# VOLUME 09 BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 0421 TO 0430 — CONTINUITY

*Appended downward. Nothing above is rewritten.*

## The ten date lines, the week, the day and the month, and both calendar numbers

**Every one of the ten files carries a date line, all ten in the seventh named year written out in full, and no date line in this batch carries the eighth named year, and the eighth named year was not added by this phase and no chapter is predicted to carry it.** Day inside the week: second, second, fourth, fourth, restarting with each month, with no exception in ten files. Weeks in the month: four, four, four. **The batch before this one ended on a two-week month, 0419 and 0420, and the month after a two-week month restarted at slot one and ran its full four, which is the first time in two batches that the run has been checked at the boundary and not inside a month.** The four hundred yards, the nine miles and the four hundred and thirty miles are walked zero times in these ten chapters and the walking of all three is on the page as something that has already happened.

**And the false premises about the tree, which are a fact about the repository and not about the page, and which the next phase inherits whether or not anybody reads this row: `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/.done` exists and the previous directory reads as finished, and there is no `.checkpoint` anywhere in the repository because `4f0360f` renamed it to `.done`.** Both false claims are in `workspace/volume-09/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` and were in `state/current.md` after Batch 0002 in the same words. **A premise handed to a phase is not a fact, and a phase that cannot reproduce one says so and does not restate it.**

## Objects, and where each of them is after these ten chapters

**The piece of paper.** One piece of paper with nothing printed on it was bought at fourpence out of the case at the near end of the boards at the top of four flights in the first week of the eighth month, was filled in by hand, was folded in three, and went inside a coat and out of that building. **It has no column, cannot be filed, and is in a coat on a back, and it is not in the second hand's corridor and was never going to be, and the reason is in a man's mouth in 0427 and not in a record.** It has not been read by anybody, has not been signed, has not been copied, has not been asked about, and nobody in this matter knows what is written on it except the man who wrote it. **A second person bought a piece of paper in 0418 and in 0420 and about four hundred buy one a year, and about four hundred a year is a great many pieces of paper and is nobody's plan.**

**The box** under the far end of those boards, about four hundred bought and unfilled blanks, eleven years, has not opened and has not moved, and is not the case. **The case** at the near end stands where it has always stood and is the only supply of the thing and is not a post. **The drawer** under the wall board is shut and has been shut for eleven years, four things in it, two never opened, and did not open. **The wall board** carries four items and not one of them is time, and the fourth is the one none of the three of them can account for, and it went back on the wall at the sixth hour in 0420 and in 0430 and did not move. **The tin** behind a boiler in a ground floor, about nine years of a man's rent in coin, is new on the page in 0428, is not a form, is not a Box of blanks, is not the coat four miles off, holds nothing written, and was not moved and is not going to be. **The leaf** in the inside pocket of a coat, about two years, with a name struck out of it in a hand that is not his mother's, is on his knees in 0425 and was not opened, and the name is in no mouth in this volume and the name of the hand that struck it is in no mouth in this volume. **The nail** in four feet of bare wall is in the wall, is a common nail driven in crooked, nothing has ever hung on it and nothing is going to this week. **The day-book** is open on its counter once a week, was not read, was not turned round, did not come off its shelf, and has four headings and no fifth column. **The rail** at the other end of the four hundred yards had nothing on it and that room is not hers and is not entered in these ten chapters. **The hook** at the end of about nine feet of corridor was empty inside a minute at the seventh hour. **The two of seventeen** are at the back of a shed in 0421, unnamed and unspoken to, and the form is on the board and goes on it again.

## People, and the separations, in the form a cold reader needs

**Marek Kest is on the page in three of these ten chapters, 0422, 0425 and 0428, in person in two of them, want visible in 0425.** Both string sweeps return 0425 alone and the answer is three, and the reason is that in 0422 and 0428 he is in the room, or in the cost of the room, and is named in neither. He holds nothing, was given nothing, reaches no stage, exercises nothing, is not thanked, is not asked anything, is not a carrier, and his reason for not reaching a stage is paid a second time zero times.

**Four separations, and all four held in these ten chapters.** A man of about sixty-one is nine miles off a building off the old river road and is neither of the two women in their boxes. A woman of about twenty-six is at the **lock end** of a cold passage nine miles off the lane off the Slade and is a third person. A woman of twenty is about four hundred and thirty miles up this river in a furnace town and is in no chapter of this batch. And the woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house is not the clerk of about fifty-three — the clerk keeps minutes about four hundred yards off the old river road and has been in that room nineteen years, and the other has been at the bottom of a stranger's stairs about twenty years and takes his rent — **and 0428 says so in its own text and the two women have never been in one building.** **Marek Kest is the only one of those three who has never given either of the other two his name, and in 0428 he is not named and in 0422 he is not named and in 0425 he is the only one of the three who is named.** The woman of about forty with a scuttle is not the woman of about twenty-six and is in no chapter of this batch, which is correct, and 0426 is a chapter at the other end of the same four hundred yards and does not put her in it. The clerk of about fifty-three and the man of about sixty-one are in the same building and neither is in these ten chapters.

**And the two men this batch is most easily going to merge, and did not.** **The young man from the shipping floor, who fills in the paper in 0423, is not the man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner, who is in 0427 and in no other chapter of this batch.** The first packs for two years, carries about four sheets a week up four flights, read the top of about six by accident, and said a thing twice in front of two people in 0403. The second carries sheets up a stair for money, is paid for a corner and not for a page, and has been the second hand's stair for about two years. They are never in one room, neither is described with the other's noun, and **the disagreement inside closed Chapter 0396 about the gender of the man who is paid by the corner is carried unrepaired on purpose, because a repair that picked would be a fourth figure where two are already unreconciled, and it is not touched here.**

## The turn, and what Movement Four inherits

**A blank is filled in. It is the smallest thing that has moved in this matter in eight volumes, it moved once, it moved into a coat, and the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter did not move, and the reason is the page's own definition and not this batch's decision.** What the next movement inherits, in a mouth and not in a record: **a thing with no column does not travel to a floor with about four hundred sheets a year going up a stair past it; it travels to a coat; the coat is on a back; the back is the cheapest party in this matter.** And the man at the foot of those sheets has been waiting nineteen years for the first thing that would ask him nothing, and it did not come, and he has said that once to a pen pot and is not going to say it again.

## The locks, each one held in all ten files

**Nobody is asked anything out loud by anybody in any of these ten chapters, and no chapter carries a question mark, and one asking was drafted in an early 0422 and caught by reading the batch for the class and rewritten so the man gives the reason unasked. Nobody is thanked, nobody is forgiven, nobody is sent for, nothing is resolved, and the arrangement is still standing.** The count of things asked out loud in this matter is seven and is printed in no chapter of this batch. The two people in their boxes stay in them and the form that put them there is not asked to be void. The question in the second of the eleven books stays a question, carries no full stop, is not printed out, and its author is not asked a second time. The figure at the end of the cold passage is not handed on, not printed in any form, and does not get a heading. The name at the end of a struck line is not printed and the name of the hand that struck it is not printed. The four who cannot make sense of a paragraph are not asked one question about a number, and the man of about fifty-five with the chain and the man of about sixty-one are neither of them one of them. A girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for, not going to start, and the girl is not given the chance to say no. The two sealed pages stay shut with two refusals between them and no third. The three stitches stay three things. The four appearances of the substituted wording keep their four ages and this volume adds no fifth. **The bill at Lowcross is unpaid, nobody is liable, it is named zero times in these ten and nine batches in a row, and this volume may name it and may not settle it, and the figure on it is not printed.** No hearing and no arrangement of one, no notice, post, commission, warrant, office, new heading over anything, or new form for anything — **and in particular no new form for a disagreement at the foot of a sheet, because there is not one and this volume's finding is that there is not going to be one.** No House, no seat and no office is named. No romance and nothing implying one, and no moment in a doorway. **The number in the head of the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand is not printed, not confirmed and not denied; the figure at the end of the cold passage is not printed; the figure on the bill at Lowcross is not printed; the count of things asked is printed nowhere in a chapter.** No calendar month name and no weekday name in any of the ten.

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three.** Plus the review gate, which has opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either. # VOLUME 09 BATCH 0004, CHAPTERS 0431 TO 0440 — THE CONTINUITY ENTRIES

*Appended downward by the phase that took `workspace/volume-09/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`. Nothing above this point is rewritten.*

## What is on the page now that was not on the page before Chapter 0431

1. **A filled-in sheet with nothing printed on it came up the second hand's corridor in 0431 and was signed at the foot like every other one that day.** It came off a pallet with about two hundred others and it was not square with them, and it had nothing printed on the top of it and about nine lines of writing on it in somebody else's hand and no space left at the foot of it for anything. It is **not** the young man from the shipping floor's sheet, which is still buttoned inside a coat and has not been up that stair and is the subject of 0432. **Nobody's name is on it and nobody's is going to be and nobody in that building knows a person filled it in.** It went into the basket at the seventh hour and the basket did not come back up.
2. **The man of about sixty-one signed a thing that wanted nothing off him for the first time in about nineteen years**, and the shape of it is now on the page: no name, no mark, no reading, no paragraph, no day, nothing to fill in, and no form to tell him what the space under the line was for. **He did not know what was in it, was not told, did not ask, and cannot ask.** His hand did not do anything different on it than it did on the first of the morning. He said a **fourth** thing to the pen pot: *Nineteen years, and I never once knew a thing did not want something. Today one did not, and I found out it is the only work I have ever done that is not asking me for anything.* The first three are his own and are carried from closed Chapter 0396 and 0427.
3. **The man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner said two things in that corridor and the first is nine words:** *More of them. That is what I am saying*, said to the wall above the sill. **The first saying this entry used to carry, *It will not be the only one. That is what I am saying*, was thirteen words and the nine-word claim was false, and the repair that took `logs/batch-0004.review.log` changed the sentence in 0431 and the wrong text is named here and deleted nowhere.** The second is **twenty-five** words: *I have signed nothing in two years that wanted nothing off me and I do not know what happens to a man when one does*, and **the twenty-two this entry used to carry was wrong and is named and not deleted.** **Neither is the finding and neither is a warning and neither is unkind, and neither is the nine words of Chapter 0425.** He did not get an answer and he was not thanked and he said he was not going to be thanked.
4. **The young man from the shipping floor's sheet did not go up that stair, and the reason is on the page in a mouth in 0432 and it is a price:** a basket that goes down at the seventh hour has been in a room with a man in it, and a coat is the cheapest place a piece of paper can be in this empire, and he has one and the man of about sixty-one has not. He has carried it about a month and has never taken it out.
5. **A sheet with no column came off a pallet and was carried up a stair by a man who is paid for a corner and not for a page, and the turn was therefore not one man.** A woman of about forty-three did it in the third week of the third month. A young man from a shipping floor did it in the first week of the eighth. A person whose name is not on the page did it in the first week of the tenth. **About four hundred a year are filled in and no two of them are the same person, and none of the three of those is the same event and none of the three is the turn.**
6. **A man of about thirty-four with a fair hand was in the rented room two streets back from the river road in 0434, and the room was his before it was Marek Kest's, and neither of them knows that and neither is going to be told.** This is the only room fact this batch adds about him and no chapter says where the room came from.
7. **He had the form with a printed heading over a ruled space, the space clean, and he held it the whole time he was in the room and did not put it down.** It came through a letter slot about two years ago and was not returned to anybody and has been out of his house for about six weeks of that two years and **he has never known who took it out and put it back.** It went out in the hand it came in. **He was given a form and not a blank, and the blank is still the one thing in this empire that could put a question in front of him without anybody asking him anything, and he does not get one, and the chapter does not say whose the asking was and neither of the two men in it found out.** The number in his head is not printed and was not confirmed and was not denied, and the chapter says that nobody confirmed it and nobody denied it. **The four words were not given to him in a mouth**, which is the thing Chapter 0414 established and which is now a standing refusal rather than a first refusal.
8. **Marek Kest did not ask, and that was the only decision in 0434 and it took him about four minutes.** A paper with nothing printed on it would have gone on the table and been found by whoever takes the room next, and there is a man in this matter he would want it to go to and he does not know where he is and **he is not going to be the one who makes him findable.** No piece of paper went on that table. The four feet of wall is still bare, the nail is still in it and still crooked, nothing has ever hung on it and nothing is going to this week. **He holds nothing at the end of the chapter that he held at the start.**
9. **A form came back to the counter at the top of four flights in 0435 with a clean ruled space under its printed heading and a crease that is not the middle of it**, put down by the man from the yard, who was not told what was in the satchel and has never been. **It was not put in the box of about four hundred blanks**, and the reason is on the page in Marn Ottery's mouth: a box is a supply, and a thing that has been filled in and brought back is not a supply, and a person who puts one in a box has done a thing on purpose in a room with two other people in it. It went down the four flights at the end of that day with nobody's name on it and it is not in any book.
10. **The clerk of about twenty-nine did not say the thing she said about a sheet with no column in 0430, and she had it ready since about the fourth hour.** Marn Ottery did not thank her, did not answer her, and did not look round. **That is the second of those in about eleven weeks, and the seven this entry carried was wrong: in 0430 the clerk spoke and it is the clerk writing none of it down that is being counted, and the first time she did that is 0424, the second week of the eighth month, and 0435 is the first week of the eleventh, which is eleven weeks and not seven and not five.**
11. **A second line went into a book of one-line weeks in 0436 and it stayed**, and it says which of the four rooms off that passage it is that stands empty the way a room stands empty when the person in it is not a person in that building. The woman of about nineteen wrote it. **The clerk of about fifty-three did not take the book away and did not put her hand flat on it, and did not tell her what to do, and did not thank her, and neither of them gave the other an order.** It has not been read again since and no third line has been added.
12. **Halla Wray gave the reason she is not a carrier, out loud, once, on her way out of the shed, and nobody asked her for it**: a class can be re-entered by whoever fills in the next form, and whoever comes in after her is in her class, and a person who can be put back is a party, and a party is a document. **She has had it since the first week and has never been able to spend it. She was not thanked for it and nobody is going to ask her for a fifth sentence.**
13. **A man from the front office told Nell Kest that all sixteen volumes go up on the second day, on two carts, and the second cart comes back empty.** She did not open the sixteenth volume, which has her own name in capitals at the head of it. **Fifteen are on a cart about four miles off and one is on a shelf behind her, unopened.** The notice with the printed ruled space on the back of it is still in her pocket and **the space is still clean after about two months**, and she did not look at it once while the cart was being loaded.
14. **The ground-floor arrangement is broken and has produced no consequence in about eleven weeks, and the consequence is the absence of one.** He came down every week and put his hand out and nothing has happened, and the reason is that a true thing said once in an empty room by a person nobody can be shown anything about is not a thing anybody can be shown either. **She put her hand on the boiler for the length of a breath and did not move the tin, and the tin is where it has been for about nine years, and she is not going to have anybody thank her for not moving it.**
15. **A man came up four flights at about the sixth hour in 0440 and bought nothing and went back down.** He is not named in that chapter. Nobody at the top of that stair has ever been told one thing about him, nobody in that building could carry it if they were told, and a woman of twenty-four is the only person who saw him arrive and is not going to say so.
16. **A clerk of about twenty-two who brings that week's returns up to a bench three times a week is on the page in 0433 for the first time.** She has been doing it about three years, is not on the rota, is not on the list of clerks, and is not named. **She refused twice** — she would not carry nine words in her head for him, and she would not be the one who says the other part of it. There is no form anywhere for a clerk who brings a bundle to be recorded as the one who refused to carry a second thing.

The day inside the week is **2 / 2 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2 / 4** across 0431 to 0440 with no exception, and the number of weeks in the month is **four, four, four**, being the tenth month at 0431 to 0434, the eleventh at 0435 to 0438 and the twelfth at 0439 and 0440. **No month in this movement came out short and no date in the volume shifted because of this batch.** The seventh named year's name is written out in full in all ten files, ten occurrences, abbreviated nowhere, and **the eighth named year's name is written zero times in these ten and is not anticipated in any of them.** **The finding, which is measured and not predicted, and which is a defect in the calendar and not in the page: the seventh named year holds forty-six weeks where the calendar gives it forty-eight, and the reason is that its fourth month came out at two weeks, being Chapters 0409 and 0410.** The sixth named year is two weeks short for the same class of reason. **So the eighth named year begins inside Movement Five and not after this movement, and this batch does not name the chapter it lands on and does not carry the name forward; it hands on two numbers, forty-six and a fourth month of two, and the decision of adding the year name belongs to the phase that writes the first chapter that falls in it and is recorded when it is taken and not before.** **The sentence this one replaces is false and is named here and deleted nowhere: *No calendar month name and no weekday name is in any of the ten files, and no chapter in any of them asserts which day of the week anything fell on.*** It was false in two of the ten files and the repair that took `logs/batch-0004.review.log` found them by opening the tree and not by reading this row: a case-insensitive sweep returns **Tuesday** at `chapter-0432.md:33` and **Wednesday** at `chapter-0440.md:21`, and they were the only two weekday names in all forty files of the volume. Both are out of the page and the same sweep now returns **zero in the ten and zero in the forty**, and no calendar month name was ever in the ten. **The finding is that a sweep which returns zero is written down in the same tone as a sweep which was never run, and a negative result in a state file is the one thing in this project's record that nothing re-measures, because a later phase reads a clean zero and inherits it as a fact about the page rather than as a result that expired when a different phase touched a file.** `this year` is written zero times in these ten files and returns **one** in this volume, at `chapter-0407.md:29`, in a closed chapter, which is the standing contradiction the outline of record denies and which is carried unrepaired.

## The four findings this batch added about the shape of a check, and none of them is about the page

**One, and it is the important one, and it is about a reconciliation.** The Batch 0003 per-file row in the state layer does not reproduce at any commit in the history, and the batch's own earlier row reproduces exactly at `70a02f3`. **The state layer's arithmetic that makes the bad row look verified — a total less its four batches equals the four hundred closed files exactly — is a check of consistency and not of accuracy, and it passes on a wrong row because the error is common to the total and to the part.** Both the right row and the wrong row give 1,091,877. **A reconciliation between a total and its parts cannot detect an error that is present in both, and the only instrument that detects it is re-measuring the parts.** The project has been treating subtraction as a check. It is not a check. It is a second copy of the assumption.

**Two, and it is the third instrument in three batches to fail in the same direction, and this failure is new.** The rule that a classifier must strip punctuation before testing a word does not go far enough, and the reason is that **a twenty-word window which straddles the edge of a date line contains no date vocabulary at all.** A date line in this manuscript is a whole clause of formula with prose on both sides of it, so a window overlapping it by three words reads as prose. The first classifier in this batch reported **239 of 250 windows against the closed page as prose and 1,391 of 1,391 against the volume's earlier chapters as prose**, and the true figures are **0 and 0**. **The check that works is positional, not lexical: mark the character span of every date line in the source file before tokenising, and call a window formula when half or more of its tokens fall inside one of those spans.** The two errors named in the last two batches both reported the page as worse than it is and this one does too, and all three are invisible to a count.

**Three, and it is the second half of the same classifier.** **Its formula bucket knows exactly one kind of formula, being a date.** This volume has at least one other, being the refrain *the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is the same as it was on the…*, which the classifier reports as prose in 0440 against 0410 and 0420 and which is **classified by hand as formula**, being a canon formula in the swept bucket, standing in six chapters of this volume, with the number itself printed in none of them. **A classifier that is given a formula list of length one will report every other formula as prose forever, and the fix is to enumerate the volume's formulas rather than to add a test.**

**Four, and it is a demonstration rather than a finding, and it is the third time this project has now shown it inside a single batch instead of inheriting it as a warning.** **A count of question marks returned zero on ten chapters that contained five real askings.** They carried full stops. Two were in 0437, one in 0438, one in 0440 and one borderline in 0432, and all five were found by reading every quoted line in the batch for the class and not by running anything against it. **Nothing this project owns can see an asking, and the instrument that finds them is the writer reading her own draft, and that is not a check and cannot be delegated and is not a review.**

## The four debts, restated in full, and this batch discharges none of them

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three.** Plus the review gate, which has opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, **and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either.** And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, which are **eighty-nine by the high-water mark, eighty-seven entries measured in the rolling ledger before the eighth enforcement and three in it now, and the sixty-three this project has been quoting is twenty-four short, which is item 90, and this batch neither corrected that figure in place nor repeated it as true.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, and it is carried and not repaired. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and a batch is not among them, and the third of those debts has a tenth instance now, and the number of instances is not a measure of the debt: nobody but the hand that wrote these ten chapters has read them.**

# THE REPAIR OF VOLUME 09 BATCH 0004 — THE CONTINUITY ENTRIES, CORRECTED IN PLACE

*Appended downward by the phase that took `logs/batch-0004.review.log`. Nothing above this point is rewritten. It opened no closed chapter, invented no fact, moved no date, closed no thread, and the count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven and did not move.*

## What the review found, and what this repair did about each, with the method named

**One, a lock, and the only true page defect of the two.** A case-insensitive sweep returns **Tuesday** at the old `chapter-0432.md:33` and **Wednesday** at the old `chapter-0440.md:21`, and they were the only weekday names in all forty files of the volume. Both are out of the page. `grep -o -iE` now returns **zero weekday names in the ten and zero in the forty**, and no calendar month name was ever in the ten. **The state layer's denial of this is corrected in place above, the wrong sentence is quoted there and is deleted nowhere, and the finding it produced is a standing: a negative result in a state file is the only figure in this record that nothing re-measures.**

**Two, the state layer denying finding one**, in the very section whose headline is that a state record which does not reproduce against its page is a premise and not a measurement. **Four of the review's six findings are this one class, and two of them are a state row asserting a sweep result that the sweep does not return.**

**Three, a count that was not made to fit.** 0431 claimed nine words over a sentence of thirteen; the state layer added "twenty-two" for a sentence of twenty-five and, in one place, called the same sentence nine words and twenty-two in the same breath. **The repair changed the sentence and not the count, because the nine-word motif is inherited from 0425 and is load-bearing in four other chapters.** The first saying is now *More of them. That is what I am saying* and is nine; the second is twenty-five. The wrong text is named above at entry 3 and in the batch summary and the character state and is deleted nowhere.

**Four, two elapsed figures in 0435 and the arithmetic of one of them.** "The thing she said in that room three weeks ago" is five weeks: 0430 is the fourth week of the ninth month and 0435 is the first week of the eleventh. "The second of those in about seven weeks" is **eleven**, and the review's own referent for it was wrong: **in 0430 the clerk of about twenty-nine spoke, and the thing being counted is her writing none of it down, whose first instance is 0424, the second week of the eighth month.** 0435 is the first week of the eleventh, which is eleven weeks from 0424 and not five from 0430. **The review was right that seven is unsupported and wrong about which pair of chapters measures it, and the repair measured it and did not take the interval from the review.**

**Five, a sweep row that does not return.** `House` case-sensitive returns **zero files** in all ten; the row said 0434 and 0439, and those two are lowercase `house` and both are the ordinary noun for a building. The row is corrected in place above and in the batch summary and the wrong file list is named and deleted nowhere.

**Six, and this is the one that is a floor and not a typo.** The protagonist's floor for Movement Four is in person in at least two of three chapters, and the state record asserted three chapters with 0433, 0434 and 0440, **but 0440 as the batch left it contained a man the chapter never identified and nothing in that file connects him to anybody.** 0433 is the floor's second clause, an absence whose cost is named in the room he is not in, and 0434 is in person, so the in-person half stood or fell on 0440 alone. **The repair made it on the page rather than weakening the record: 0440 now says it is the third time in his life he has come up those four flights, and the stair has no door at the foot of it, and he has never knocked on that stair, which is the man of 0407, who came up for the second time in his life and never knocked, and 0413 has a woman naming him at that counter outright.** He is still not named in 0440 and no lock is touched. **The floor is now met by reading, which is what the outline requires and what a string cannot do, and the strings are unchanged: `Kest` returns 0434, `thirty-eight` returns 0433 and 0434, the union is two against an answer of three, and the third is 0440.**

**Seven, which the review did not find.** **0434 dated a counter visit four months ago; the calendar gives twenty-seven weeks between 0407 and 0434, and 0413 calls the same visit six weeks.** It is about six months. **Three elapsed figures in ten chapters, two found and one missed, and the class is the standing this project already has about a different instrument: a figure written in a chapter is a claim about the calendar, the calendar is the one countable thing in this manuscript, and all three were checked by reading.**

## The figures, re-measured after the last edit to any chapter, and the blind spot of each

**Words, `sed 's/[[:space:]]*$//' file | wc -w`, per file, one file at a time and never with a glob.** 1,478 / 1,226 / 1,427 / 1,540 / 1,263 / 1,114 / 1,181 / 1,084 / 1,179 / 1,273, the batch **12,765**; Volume 09 **56,865** across forty files; the manuscript **1,148,742** per-file sum and **1,148,739** by concatenation; the four hundred closed files **1,091,877** and **1,091,874** by concatenation, unchanged, because this repair opened no closed chapter. **The figures before it — 1,482 / 1,227 / 1,427 / 1,539 / 1,263 / 1,114 / 1,181 / 1,084 / 1,179 / 1,220, the batch 12,716, Volume 09 56,816, the manuscript 1,148,693 and 1,148,690 — were correct for the manuscript as the batch left it, are named here and deleted nowhere, and the difference is exactly the five edited chapters: 0431 minus four, 0432 minus one, 0434 plus one, 0435 plus two, 0440 plus fifty-one.** The blind spot is that a word count cannot see whether the words are the same words, and the two tokenizers below are the instrument that would have caught a repair that introduced one.

**The shared-run window, at twenty words, both tokenizers, both scopes, and the classifier corrected positionally rather than lexically.** Whitespace-tokenised against the four hundred closed files: **0 shared windows of prose.** Punctuation-stripped and lowercased: **0.** Against Volume 09's own thirty earlier chapters: **0 prose.** Internally, the ten files against themselves: **0.** **The five sentences this repair wrote were measured with the same window before they were accepted, and none of them re-prints anything, which matters more here than anywhere else in the project because a repair is the one phase whose edits land on top of prose that has already been swept once.**

**The string sweeps, after the repair.** `Kest` → 0434. `thirty-eight` → 0433, 0434. The union is **two** and the floor is **three**, and the third is 0440 and is now on the page. `blanks` → 0433, 0435, 0440, **six occurrences, unchanged**, none at the lock end of the cold flags, and the 0416 double-use at that end is untouched at two. `Roll`, `public roll`, `charter`, `coalition`, `mountain`, `convention` → zero. `House` case-sensitive → **zero files**, and lowercase `house` → 0434, 0439, both the ordinary noun for a building. `Lowcross` → 0433, once. `this year` → zero in these ten and **one** in the volume at `chapter-0407.md:29`, in a closed chapter, carried unrepaired. `this volume` → zero in these ten. **Weekday names → zero in the ten and zero in the forty, against two before the repair. Question marks → zero in the ten, and the five real askings the batch drafted remain out of the page, and this repair read every quoted line in the five edited chapters for the class and introduced none.**

**The calendar, unchanged by the repair.** The day inside the week is **2 / 2 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 2 / 4** across 0431 to 0440 with no exception, the month is **four, four, four**, the seventh named year's name is written out in full in all ten files and the eighth is written zero times and is not anticipated. **The seventh named year still holds forty-six weeks where the calendar gives it forty-eight, and the fourth month of that year still came out at two weeks, and the eighth named year therefore still begins inside Movement Five, and this repair still does not name the chapter it lands on and does not carry the name forward.** The two calendar numbers handed on are unchanged: forty-six, and a fourth month of two.

## The five findings this repair added, and none of them is about the page

**One, and it is the one this repair is really about: a lock that is written down as held has been checked by nobody.** Two locks this project states in its own prompts were broken in the batch the prompts sent — a weekday name, twice — and both were found by running a sweep, and neither was found by reading the chapter, because a weekday is two words in a sentence and a reader does not stop on it. **A lock is a thing this project asserts about itself and the assertion is the only part of the record with no instrument attached to it.**

**Two: a stated count of words in a quoted sentence is a measurement and it is the one measurement nothing here runs.** The sentence is in a chapter, the count is in a state file, and nothing joins them. Nine claimed for thirteen, twenty-two for twenty-five, and once nine and twenty-two for the same sentence in one clause. **Counting it is four seconds of work and no chapter is written with it.**

**Three: an elapsed figure names an event before it names an interval.** The review found seven weeks where the calendar gives eleven and attributed it to 0430, and 0430 is the week the clerk spoke rather than the week she wrote nothing down. **The finding arrived with a wrong premise inside it, and a repair that had taken the interval from the review instead of measuring it would have written a number into a chapter that was right for the wrong reason, which is the failure class this project has now found in four consecutive batches.**

**Four: a repair is the one phase whose edits land on prose that has already been swept, so its sentences are the ones most likely to re-print something, and none of the five written here does.** The window was run before acceptance and not after, and the standing is that it is run before, because a re-printed passage caught after a commit is a second commit.

**Five, and it is a demonstration: a state layer can carry a clean negative for one full phase and be wrong, and the reason is that a negative is the only figure in the record that expires silently.** Every other figure in this project is bound to a phase that measured it and is visibly stale when a later phase touches the page. **A clean zero has no date on it, and this is the third batch in a row to find a figure that was correct when written and wrong when inherited, and the first one that was a zero.**

## The four debts, unchanged, and this repair discharges none of them

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three.** Plus the review gate, which has fallen back to the writing agent on every phase the history records and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, **and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either.** And the open items that are not plot, which are **eighty-nine by the high-water mark, eighty-seven entries measured in the rolling ledger before the eighth enforcement and three in it now, and the sixty-three this project has been quoting is twenty-four short, which is item 90, and this repair neither corrected that figure in place nor repeated it as true.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, and it is carried and not repaired. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero.**

# VOLUME 09 BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 0401 TO 0410 — THE PEOPLE AT FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN CHAPTERS, AND WHAT EACH OF THEM IS STILL REFUSED

*Appended downward by the phase that took `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status about any person above is changed.** This batch wrote ten chapters into `chapters/volume-09/` and created that directory, and it opened no chapter of any closed volume. **Every person below is a person and not a device, and each is named by age or by what they do at a counter, and no chapter used one of them as a function.** Two minor people enter in this batch and are on the page now: a man of about forty-four who has loaded on a shipping floor for nineteen years, and a man of about thirty-one with a book who comes to a door about a freeholder's wish. Neither is merged with anybody and neither is examinable by anybody.

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist, in three chapters of ten and in person in all three: 0404, 0407 and 0409.** **The floor was counted by reading and not by a string, and the sentence that puts him in each room is named here so a reader can check the count instead of taking it.** 0404 — "He has a name, Marek Kest, and he is thirty-eight, and what he does for a living is go through contracts and inheritances for an empire that does not pay anybody for going through them." 0407 — "He has a name, Marek Kest, and he is thirty-eight, and there is no office behind him of any kind and no money, no roll and no leave, and nothing he could hand anybody in exchange for being let up a stair." 0409 — "He is thirty-eight, his name is Marek Kest, and there is no office, no money, no roll, no leave and no certification behind him, and none of those five is on its way." **His want is visible in 0404, where the leaf is in the inside pocket of his coat, and in 0407, where a woman of twenty-four tells him at a counter that he has a shape and no thing.** He holds nothing and was given nothing this batch: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage, no certification, no commission, no warrant, no heading over his name, no second copy of anything, and no reading and no exercise of the thing he is able to do. **The name of the hand that struck the line out of the leaf is not printed in any of the ten chapters, and the name that belongs at the end of it is not printed, and his reason for not reaching a stage is referred to once and not paid a second time.** He is not thanked, nobody asked him anything, and the woman about four miles off at the top of a stair is still not told that he said a reason out loud. **And the two string sweeps were run and are wrong in the standing's way, and it is recorded here because this is the first Volume 09 measurement of it: `grep -l 'Kest'` returns three files and is right, and `grep -l 'thirty-eight'` returns five, of which 0405 is Rennick Adley and 0410 is a woman of about thirty-eight who buys a blank, so the age sweep returns two files the man is not in.**

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the fourth counter, two years, in 0402 and 0410 and once at the far end of 0401.** Nobody has put a question to her in this batch and nobody has put one to her in two years, and **her undertaking is live, unretired and unexercised, and its date is not said out loud in any of the ten chapters and is not said out loud by this state record either, which is the standing and is honoured here.** What the batch found out about her, and it is two things and not a third: **in 0402 she says out loud, once, that she does not know what the twenty minutes are for, and that she does not know whether she is being careful or getting ready, and that she will not decide it in a room with Marn Ottery in it — and the decision about whether she trusts a colleague's judgment is not made and is not made here.** In 0407 she says the sentence the volume is built on, to a man, in a room, about the exact width of a name, and is not thanked, and in 0410 she says a question-shaped sentence that carries no mark at the end of it and is not answered. **She is not an organiser, not a clerk with a title, not a person given a post, not a person somebody has asked, and not a person who has been made a decision about, and the counter is shut at the sixth hour in two of these chapters and the twenty minutes are still twenty minutes and are not a decision and are not on anything.**

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the other end of the boards, eleven years, not a lawyer, in 0401, 0407 and 0410 and present in 0402 and 0403.** She says the plainest sentence anybody has said on that floor in eleven years and is not thanked and says in terms that she is not going to be. **In 0407, for the first time in eleven years, she says nothing at all while a thing is said in her room, and she notices that she has said nothing, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a clerk to be recorded as the one who did not speak.** The box was not opened, the drawer was not opened, and the fourth item on the board is still the one none of the three of them can account for. **Her want is unchanged: that the two women at that counter go on being the only two who know what is in that drawer, and that the arrangement that makes that true keeps standing, and it is still not on the page as a want and is not printed as one here.**

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the Company's book-keeper, four hundred yards up the lane off the Slade, in 0405 and in no other chapter of this batch.** Wants the rest of a sentence and has not asked and is not going to, and it is now the **ninth** time the four lines have come out the same — **and the figure this row carried was the twelfth, and the repair that took `logs/batch-0001.review.log` changed it, because Chapter 0405 is dated the second day of the first week of the third month and twelve weekly copies of four lines cannot start inside the seventh named year: the eleventh of them would fall eleven weeks before that date, which is before the first month of the year. The first of the nine is in the first month, which is also the month the sentence stopped in a doorway, and the other eight weeks is what the chapter now says. The wrong figure is named here and deleted nowhere.** He is thirty-eight, he is not the protagonist, and no chapter of this batch merges the two men in a sentence, a room, an age or a sweep, and the age sweep itself puts them in the same column and is wrong. The four hundred yards is not walked, no blank is carried up it, the second half of the sentence does not arrive, and he moved his own four lines to the foot of his own sheet and was not thanked for it and did not say a word about it.

**The woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house, in 0404 and 0409 from above the second stair and in 0406 from her own ground floor.** About twenty years at the bottom of those stairs, never once above the second one, takes a rent, has never had his name and has never given him hers. **She is the only person in the batch who says a true thing to Marek Kest twice, and in 0404 it is about a nail and in 0409 it is the plainest statement of his position anybody has made to him: there is no form in this empire for a man to be given a piece of paper, and the reason nothing has ever been sent to him is that nothing has ever been sent to anybody.** **She is not the clerk of about fifty-three. The clerk keeps minutes about four hundred yards off the old river road and has been in that room nineteen years; this one has never been inside that building, does not know what was said in it, and is not in that building in any of the ten chapters, and the two women are never in one room. **The rest of that sentence is withdrawn by the repair that took `logs/batch-0001.review.log` and is named here and deleted nowhere: it said that no chapter states or denies a distance between any two of the three people the state layer keeps apart, and 0406 states one, in this woman's own ground floor, with a man of about thirty-eight on the floor above her, and the outline states the same distance itself. The three are that clerk, this woman and Marek Kest, and Marek Kest is the only one of the three who has never given either of the other two his name — which is the sentence that makes the clause beside it impossible, because it can only be true if the other two have given each other theirs, and that is the merge the fourth separation forbids. The fourth separation itself holds and is checkable: the clerk of about fifty-three is in none of these ten files.**

**The clerk of about twenty-nine, at the other end of two joined tables and at that counter, in 0401, 0402, 0403, 0405, 0407, 0409 and 0410.** She said the number of the fourth item out loud to two people in eleven years and was asked nothing, and she said in 0401 that the not asking was not a courtesy, because there is no form anywhere in which a person may be told what a thing on a board is going to be used for, and that politeness makes a shape when there is nothing to be polite with. **She has still said no second thing in that room and she kept that in 0403 and in 0410, and she refuses to pass a sentence on to a person who has not asked her for it, and she has stopped putting her pen away early.** She is not thanked.

**The young man from the shipping floor, in 0403, and no other chapter of this batch.** A statement made twice in front of two people has two people attached to it, and he does not take it back and does not say it a third time, and he has no room to be served in, and the box is where it was.

**The courier, in 0401, 0403, 0405 and 0410, and in no other chapter of this batch.** A man from the yard with a satchel. **He cannot be told anything and asks nothing in any of the four chapters he is in, and in 0405 there is nobody in that lane who could tell anybody anything about him, and in 0403 the two of them go in opposite directions and neither knows the other's name, and he is not examinable by anybody in this matter.** He is not thanked and was not thanked.

**The man in the felt apron, in 0408.** He says the one true thing he has carried for four years — that a sheet came down that passage once in four years and went back up, and that nobody has ever asked about the going back up — and then keeps going up and down that stone four times a day, and is not thanked.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the lock end of about four hundred yards of cold flags, in 0408 and no other chapter of this batch.** Four years at that lock, never one of the three clerks, never sent for, thanked or handed a sheet. **She is at the lock and the room with the rail is at the other end of those same four hundred yards and is not hers, and the rail has nothing on it; this row's claim that she was at the end of that passage and that the room at the far end was not entered was false against the page, is named here and deleted nowhere, and Chapter 0408 is written from the lock end, walks the four hundred yards zero times, and does not put the woman of about forty with a scuttle in that passage, because a reference to her was written and cut.** Her figure is on no paper, has gone up, is not given away in any form, is not handed on, and does not get a heading.

**The woman of about forty with a scuttle, in no chapter of this batch, and that is correct.** **The claim that stood above, that she is in none of Volume 08's fifty files, was false against the page and is named here and deleted nowhere: she is in fourteen of the four hundred closed files, and Chapter 0362 is named for her and is set in the room with the rail from its first paragraph to its last.** What survives the correction is the instruction that was always the point, and this batch did not breach it: a Volume 09 writer may not give her a room, a trade, a week or a history the page has not given her, may not discover her where she was not, and may not put her in a passage she was not written into. **She is not the woman of about twenty-six and the two are never in one room.**

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, the man of about sixty-one, the clerk of about fifty-three, the woman of about nineteen, Halla Wray, Nell Kest, the woman of about thirty-five next door, the woman of about thirty-five with a day-book, the woman of about twenty-two in her second season, the man of about twenty-six paid by the corner, and the man of about fifty-five with the chain: none of them is in any of the ten chapters, and every one of them stands exactly as the handoff left them.** The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand is not asked anything and the number in his head is not printed. The man of about sixty-one's figures were used once in 0407, unasked for, and were not confirmed, not added to and not put in a book, and he was not asked anything. The clerk of about fifty-three is not in any of the ten chapters and is not in that house. Halla Wray is not in any of the ten chapters and the form that put her where she is is not asked to be void by anybody. **Nell Kest is not in any of the ten chapters, the four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times, and no chapter put her in a room with a blank or with the woman who carries one.**
