# THE REPAIR OF VOLUME 09 BATCH 0001 AT `logs/batch-0001.review.log`, AND WHAT CHANGED ABOUT THESE PEOPLE, WHICH IS ALMOST NOTHING

**Nothing about anybody in this volume changed except three things that were wrong and one clause that was wrong, and the person a Batch 0002 writer inherits is the person the batch wrote.** No want was resolved, no refusal was lifted, no name was printed, no undertaking was exercised or retired, and nobody was thanked, forgiven or sent for. **The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven and is unmoved.** The full entry is `reviews/volume-09-batch-0001.md`; this is the part of it a writer opening this file for the first time needs.

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the Company's book-keeper.** The row above carries the correction: the count of his four lines is **nine, not twelve**, and the reason is arithmetic against his own date line rather than taste, because a week is a week and eleven of them do not fit inside the time the batch has covered. **He is thirty-eight and so is the protagonist and the age sweep has always put them in one column; the repair added a third thirty-eight to the batch's false positives in the making and removed one, and the woman of about thirty-eight who buys a blank at fourpence in 0410 is still not either man and never was.**

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist.** Three chapters, in person in all three, want visible in two: unchanged, and the sentence that puts him in each room is still the sentence that puts him there. **The repair took one asking out of his mouth at `chapter-0409.md:23` and put a statement in its place, and the chapter is better for it, because a man who has not asked anybody anything for two years does not start at the seventh hour; what he does is finish a sentence she has already started and wait.** He still holds nothing, is still given nothing, is still not thanked, and the name at the end of the struck line is still not printed.

**The woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house.** Still in 0404, 0406 and 0409, still about twenty years at the bottom of those stairs, still never had his name and never given him hers. **The repair removed two sentences from 0409 that pointed at a second woman in that building — "Not to the woman in the room you do not know about and I have never seen" is now "Not to me. Not to anybody in this row, and I have watched this row for twenty years" — and changed "on a floor tonight" to "in a room tonight", eleven words before she distinguishes a room from a doorway.** A person who has been carrying a plain sentence for nine years and has never been thanked for it is a person, and a person who is also the fourth item on somebody's board is a person, and the repair took nothing away from her and gave her nothing.

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, and Tamsin Rook, twenty-four.** Both unchanged in every particular, and one sentence changed. **Marn Ottery's speech in `chapter-0402.md` no longer ends "Say the other one. The one underneath. Say it once and I will leave you alone about it." It ends with her stating that she has decided not to ask, and setting what she will do if the thing is said anyway, and Tamsin Rook says it, unasked, four lines later, and says that saying it now is the first time she has said it.** That is a better scene than the one it replaces and it is the same scene, and the only thing removed from it was the asking. **Marn Ottery's three true things are still three before 0407 and the fourth is still in the first week of the second month, which is Chapter 0401, and the figure this batch's own record gave for that week was wrong and is corrected in the row above and in the sweeps.**

**The clerk of about twenty-nine at the other end of two joined tables.** Five named files, six at most, and "seven of the ten" is withdrawn and named. `chapter-0403.md` no longer calls her a man. She has still said no second thing in that room and no third thing to him either.

**The three people the state layer keeps apart.** The clerk of about fifty-three is in none of these ten files and `grep -rn 'fifty-five' chapters/volume-09/` returns nothing, which is why the fourth separation holds; the clause that forbade the ground-floor woman and Kest sharing a room is withdrawn, with the reason and the four checks that decide it in `state/current.md`. **No character was merged with another by this repair, and the repair considered two substitutions that would have merged people and refused both, which is the only interesting thing a repair of a merge rule can do.**

# VOLUME 09 BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 0411 TO 0420 — THE PEOPLE AT FOUR HUNDRED AND TWENTY CHAPTERS, AND WHAT EACH OF THEM IS STILL REFUSED

*Appended downward by the phase that took `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status about any person above is changed.** This batch wrote ten files into `chapters/volume-09/` and opened no chapter of any closed volume. **Every person below is a person and not a device, and each is named by age or by what they do at a counter, and no chapter used one of them as a function.** Three minor people enter in this batch and are on the page now: **the man who comes in on the second of the two days at the counter, who is not a lawyer and has not been one for about nine years; a woman of about thirty who buys a blank at fourpence in 0420; and a woman of about twenty-nine who buys one in 0418.** None is merged with anybody and none is examinable by anybody. **And this batch is the first Volume 09 batch in which a person is refused by the page and not only by a rule: the woman of about nineteen says the one true thing she has and is not thanked for it, and the foreman of fifty-one is told what she costs and is not thanked for it, and neither of them is thanked because nobody in this matter is.**

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist, in three chapters of ten and in person in two of the three: 0413, 0417 and 0420.** **The floor was counted by reading and not by a string, and the sentence that puts him in each room is named here so a reader can check the count instead of taking it.** 0413, absent, his name in a clerk's mouth and the cost of his absence named in the room he is not in — *"I have known the price of it since a man came up those four flights on the fourth day of the third week of the third month, and Marek Kest is the name on him, and he did not buy a copy of anything and went back down them, and it is six weeks since and I have had all six of them to work out what he came for."* 0417, in person — *"He is thirty-eight and his name is Marek Kest, and he is not at a stage and is not going to arrive at one."* 0420, in person — *"He has a name, Marek Kest, and he is not a lawyer, and there is no office behind him of any kind and no money, no list, no leave and no certification, and none of those five is on its way."* **His want is visible in 0417 and in 0420, in the leaf and in the page he carries and in what they used to say at the end of that line, and the name and the name of the hand that struck it are printed in none of the ten.** He holds nothing and was given nothing this batch: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage, no certification, no commission, no warrant, no heading over his name, no second copy of anything, and no reading and no exercise of the thing he is able to do. **He is not thanked, nobody asked him anything, and his reason for not reaching a stage is referred to nowhere in these ten chapters and paid a second time zero times. And he is not a carrier: 0417 is the chapter in which the cheapest party in this matter is told his price out loud, in a rented room, by a person who has been carrying a thing of her own for nine years, and he gives her nothing back, he does not repeat it, and he holds nothing at the end of the evening that he did not hold at the start of it.** The two string sweeps were run and **both were right this time, which is a result and not a clearance**: `grep -l 'Kest'` returns three and is him, and `grep -l 'thirty-eight'` returns two and both are him, and the reason the age sweep had nothing to be wrong about is that no other person in these ten files is thirty-eight.

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the fourth counter, two years, and 0418 is hers alone.** Nobody has put a question to her in this batch and nobody has put one to her in two years, and **her undertaking is live, unretired and unexercised, and its date is not said out loud in any of the ten chapters and is not said out loud by this state record either, which is the standing and is honoured here. And in 0418 she has done the one thing in two years that was hers alone to do, which is stop coming in twenty minutes early, and Marn Ottery tells her out loud that what she has been standing ready for is nothing, and she says in the room that she is not going to decide anything about it in that room, and the decision about whether she trusts a colleague's judgment is not made in this chapter and is not made in this file.** She is not an organiser, not a clerk with a title, not a person given a post, not a person somebody has asked, and not a person who has been made a decision about, and the twenty minutes are gone and are not a decision and are not on anything, and the counter shuts at the sixth hour.

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the other end of the boards, eleven years, not a lawyer, in 0413 and 0420.** **In 0413 she refuses the finding the man on the second of the two days brings her — that the courier is a carrier and is the cheapest person in the building — on the ground that a person in this matter is a person who can be shown what a thing is going to be used for, and she names the price of her own refusal out loud in front of a third person, which is that a man who is not examinable cannot be examined on her behalf either.** In 0420 she refuses a second time: the clerk of about twenty-nine hands her a reason, and she says she is not going to use it and is not going to be the one who turns a reason into a favour. **In both chapters she is the only person in that room who can say what a blank is for and she said it out loud once, in the first week of the second month, and nobody thanked her, and she has said in terms that she is not going to be.** The box was not opened and did not move, the drawer was not opened, the case is still not the box, the fourth item is still the one none of the three of them can account for, and **her want is unchanged: that the two women at that counter go on being the only two who know what is in that drawer, and that the arrangement that makes that true keeps standing, and it is still not on the page as a want and is not printed as one here.**

**Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the Company's book-keeper, in no chapter of this batch, and that is correct.** The age sweep did not find him this time because he is not in these ten files, and **he is thirty-eight and so is the protagonist and the two men are in no chapter, no sentence, no room and no sweep together, and nothing this batch wrote about the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand could be read as a description of the book-keeper, because the book-keeper copies figures on somebody's list and the fair hand is a man who is findable and married and therefore cannot be a carrier.** The four hundred yards is not walked and the second half of the sentence in the doorway has not arrived.

**The woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house, in 0417 and in no other chapter of this batch.** About twenty years at the bottom of those stairs, never once above the second one, takes a rent, has never had his name and has never given him hers. **In 0417 she comes up those stairs with nothing in her hands for the third time in about two years and the second time in five weeks, and she says the smallest true thing about price anybody has said to him: he is the cheapest party in this matter, a practice that works by being cheap will find him every time without being told to, and the bill for being unfindable is not his and has never been his.** She says the four hundred and thirty miles is not going to be walked on his account and that this is not a kindness, and that there is a girl at the end of it who is not being paid and is not going to ask. **He gives her nothing back: he has watched her decide twice that week whether to say what she has carried for nine years, and he does not give her a third opening, and he has not asked her and is not going to, and the two of them are left with a thing between them with no form on it, which is the only kind either of them has ever had. She is not thanked, and she is not the clerk of about fifty-three, and the two women are in different buildings in different chapters and no distance between them is stated or denied.**

**The clerk of about fifty-three, nineteen years in one room off the old river road, in 0411 and no other chapter of this batch.** Writes the minutes, advises nobody, asks nothing. **In 0411 she resists the woman of about nineteen who keeps them and says out loud that she has entered about nine hundred minutes and that not one of them has an answer in it, and that a minute with an answer in it is an order, and that an order has a name at the end of it, and that it is why nobody in that building can be told what a carrier is — and then refuses to be told it, having thought about it for four minutes and a half and decided not to be told it in about a second.** She says the not asking was not a courtesy in 0401 and she is not thanked in 0411 either, and she tells the nineteen-year-old plainly that she is not going to thank her, because the girl has just handed her a thing she had already refused. **Her own question on the shelf under the window with no pane in it is in the room and is not printed out, and it has her name at the foot of it and does not end, and nobody in these ten chapters asks its author a second thing about it.** The man of about sixty-one is in the same building and goes past that door twice in one hour and is asked nothing and is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph.

**The woman of about nineteen who keeps minutes under her, in 0411 and no other chapter of this batch.** Six weeks in the job and on no list in that building, which is the arrangement that lets her sit in that room and be nobody. **She says out loud, once, in a room, to one person, that a carrier is the half of a record that never gets written down and that she is the other half, and that her whole labour is the making of the record a carrier is spared, and that the nine hundred minutes are the other side of a person carrying something up a stair. Nobody thanked her and she said in advance that she was not going to be thanked for it and was not going to be told it was useful, and nobody did, and nobody asked her one thing, and she is not asked a second time about anything this batch.** The minute of the week before says the room stood empty and the room was not empty and she was in it, and the page is right about the room and wrong about her, and that is the only kind of page anybody has ever written about her.

**The man of about fifty-five with the chain, in 0412 and no other chapter of this batch.** He says the volume's sentence out loud, once, in the one phrasing the batch uses, and then says the price of a woman of fifty-one who is inside a box a class entered, and nobody thanks him and he does not expect to be thanked. **He is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph and never has been, and 0415 says so out loud about him by name, and that is the whole of the separation and it was made in a mouth rather than in a record.**

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman in a box entered by a class, in 0412 and no other chapter of this batch.** **A person in that shed tells her out loud, as a fact, that she is not a carrier and cannot be one, and the reason is that a class is a heading and a heading is a company and whoever fills in the next form goes in the same box, and a carrier who can be re-entered is a carrier who is already a party. And then he says the price: a foreman inside a box entered by a class is the cheapest person in this matter, cheapness is the price, and the price of her is that she is reachable, and he did not have to do anything to reach her.** She gives no answer to it, she is not thanked for her four sentences, nobody asks her for a fifth, the form on the board goes back on it on the second of the two days next week, the form that put her there is not asked to be void by anybody, the two of seventeen at the back of that shed are not named in the chapter and are not spoken to, and the second half of a sentence she began in a doorway has still not arrived and the lamp is still not lit until about the seventh hour.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, in 0414 and no other chapter of this batch.** **He tells a woman of about thirty-five who keeps a day-book that he is a carrier and cannot be one, and he does not give her the four words, and she works the reason out standing at that counter and does not say she has worked it out, and that not saying is the cost and there is nowhere in that lane to put down a thing a person has worked out on her own account.** He is not on anybody's list that day, he is not one of his four days a season, he tells her nothing about them, and **the number in his head is not printed, not confirmed and not denied.** He knocked on nothing, she knocked twice in eleven years and has not knocked a third time, and neither of them starts anything.

**The woman of about thirty-five who keeps a day-book, in 0414 and no other chapter of this batch, and in one of the three chapters of the volume that carry her.** About nine years of a book with four printed headings over it — room, week, name, what was paid — and no fifth column ruled by anybody. **She is the woman next door and has been next door eleven years and she is not the woman at the foot of the four steps and never has been, and that separation is on the page and was made in closed Chapter 0369 and is not disturbed by this chapter.** The book was not read, was not turned round, and did not come off that shelf. She is not thanked.

**The man of about sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two, in 0415 and 0419 and no other chapter of this batch.** Cannot read a paragraph and has never once pretended to. **In 0415 a man of about twenty-six tells him out loud that he has been a carrier for nineteen years, that the only thing he has never done is read a heading, that this is the trade and not a gap in him, and that the reason a carrier is cheap is that an entry goes where there is nowhere to keep an entry, so that a man like him is cheap and a man who could be shown a list of the things he has carried would be an expensive one.** He answers once, to the pot and not to a man, and the sentence is his own from closed Chapter 0396. **In 0419 he says the substance of it a second time in his own words and adds that the second thing is not his to date, because a month put on it in a corridor makes it a thing with a day on it and not a thing in a man, and the basket is the only part of any of it the young man is paid for.** **His own figures are not used in these ten chapters: about nine hundred sheets a year and about nine of them a year that are not what the person above him wrote are neither confirmed nor denied nor put in a book, and he is asked nothing, and the nine miles is walked zero times, and the four men in that bay are still not asked one question about a number.** He is not thanked.

**The man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner, in 0415 and 0419 and in no other chapter of this batch, and this row carries a disagreement that is not closed here.** **The batch prompt's card for 0415 calls him the young woman and its card for 0419 calls him the man, and `outline/volume-09.md` says *a man of about twenty-six paid by the corner* in two places and the state layer says it, and closed Chapter 0396 says *The young man who carries the sheets away* in its eleventh line and *The young woman took his hand off the sill. He was twenty-six.* in its thirty-first, and 0396 is closed and no phase may open it.** The page and the outline of record are with the two, so both chapters are written with a man, and **the disagreement inside 0396 is carried unrepaired on purpose, because a repair that picked would be a fourth figure where two are already unreconciled, which is the same standing that carries item 83.** He is paid for a corner and not for a page and the basket goes down and does not come back up. **He told the man of about sixty-one in closed Chapter 0396 that a second true thing was in him and that it would come out in about four months, and he entered the four months as a guess and said so at the time; it does not arrive, and in 0419 he dates that out loud, in a mouth, standing in a corridor, and says the guess was a guess and that whatever was in him that morning is not in him now and that he is not going to work out which day it went and there is nobody he is going to tell.** The four months is not described as exact anywhere.

**The man in the felt apron, in 0416 and no other chapter of this batch.** **He resists the woman at the lock by saying the word wrong — a carrier is a person on a list — and holds that a list is not the same as being asked, and then sets out the trade: a sheet came down that passage once in four years and went back up with the space clean, and there is no rule on that passage about a blank, and there is not going to be one, and the reason is that no rule was ever needed.** He is refused a sentence for the first time in four years and does not ask again, and he is not thanked.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the lock end of about four hundred yards of cold flags, in 0416 and no other chapter of this batch.** Four years at that lock, never one of the three clerks. **She says out loud that the reason nobody has ever asked her what she carries is that being asked is being made a party and a party is a document, and that she is the only person at that end who could turn a thing coming into something that costs, and she decides at about the fifth hour to keep the lock knowing exactly that, and the not moving is the whole of what it cost.** Her figure is on no paper, has gone up, is not given away in any form in any sentence, is not handed on, and does not get a heading. **The four hundred yards is walked zero times, the rail at the other end is not hers and is empty, and the woman of about forty with a scuttle is in no chapter of this batch.**

**The woman of about forty with a scuttle, in no chapter of this batch, and that is correct.** **The claim that stood above, that she is in none of Volume 08's fifty files, was false against the page and is named here and deleted nowhere: she is in fourteen of the four hundred closed files, and Chapter 0362 is named for her and is set in the room with the rail from its first paragraph to its last.** What survives the correction is the instruction that was always the point, and this batch did not breach it: a Volume 09 writer may not give her a room, a trade, a week or a history the page has not given her, may not discover her where she was not, and may not put her in a passage she was not written into — **and Chapter 0416 is a chapter at the other end of the same four hundred yards and does not put her in it.** She is not the woman of about twenty-six and the two are never in one room.

**The courier, in 0413 and no other chapter of this batch.** A man from the yard with a satchel, and **in 0413 he is the chapter.** He goes up and down those four flights more often than anybody at that counter has been standing at them, he is set down on the boards and not told what is in the thing, and **a man on the floor says out loud that he is a carrier, that he has been one for longer than Marn Ottery has had those boards, and that he is the cheapest person who walks into that building because he cannot be asked, cannot be asked twice and cannot remember — and Marn Ottery refuses the whole of it, and says out loud that if he is a carrier then he is a person in this matter and a person in this matter is a person who can be shown what a thing is going to be used for, and that the price of that refusal is that he cannot be examined on her behalf either.** **He is not examinable, he is not made examinable, he is not thanked, and nothing in this batch gives him a name or an age or a room, and he could not have told anybody what he had held that day if a man had stood at the bottom of that stair with a form in his hand.** He is not merged with the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand and is not merged with anybody else who is findable.

**The clerk of about twenty-nine, at the other end of two joined tables, in 0411, 0413, 0418 and 0420.** **In 0418 she says nothing while a true thing is said in her room and keeps the promise she made about a second thing, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a clerk to be recorded as the one who did not speak. In 0420 she says the reason nobody can be told what a thing on a board is going to be used for, and then says that a thing nobody can be shown is a thing that cannot be asked about, and that a thing which cannot be asked about is the only kind of paper this empire has that nobody can be held to — and she gives the figures, about four years for the fourth item and about eleven for the box, and says they are the same shape and not the same age, and Marn Ottery had said the two figures in the wrong order on purpose.** She says at the end that this is the first thing she has said in that room in about four years that she has not known the end of, and that there will not be a second thing about it. She is not thanked.

**Nell Kest, the man in the felt apron's building, the two of seventeen, the girl of seventeen, the reader of seventeen, the woman of about twenty-two in her second season, and the two women in their boxes: none of them is in a chapter of this batch except the two of seventeen, who are in 0412 at the back of a shed, unnamed and unspoken to, and the woman of about twenty-two is not in any of the ten.** **Nell Kest is named in 0417 only as a girl about four hundred and thirty miles up this river who is not being paid and is not going to ask, the four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times, and no chapter of this batch put her in a room with a blank or with a woman who carries one.** A girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for, and not going to start.

### The four debts, restated in full at Batch 0002, and this batch discharges none of them

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three.** Plus the review gate, which has opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records, and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked, and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, **and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either.** And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, which are now **eighty-nine by the high-water mark, eighty-seven entries measured in the rolling ledger before the eighth enforcement and three in it now, and the sixty-three this project has been quoting is twenty-four short, which is item 90, and this batch neither corrected that figure in place nor repeated it as true.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file and no phase may edit it. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and the third debt now has an eighth instance because nobody but the hand that wrote these ten chapters has read them.**

# VOLUME 09 BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 0421 TO 0430 — THE PEOPLE AT FOUR HUNDRED AND THIRTY CHAPTERS, AND WHAT EACH OF THEM IS STILL REFUSED

*Appended downward by the phase that took `workspace/volume-09/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status about any person above is changed.** This batch wrote ten files into `chapters/volume-09/` and opened no chapter of any closed volume. **Every person below is a person and not a device, and each is named by age or by what they do at a counter, and no chapter used one of them as a function.** Two minor people enter on the page in this batch and neither is examinable by anybody: **the young man from the shipping floor, who is the point of view of 0423 and who fills in the piece of paper, and the tin behind a boiler, which is not a person and is listed here only because it is the one new object the movement put on the page.**

**Marek Kest, thirty-eight, the protagonist, in three chapters of ten and in person in two of the three: 0422, 0425 and 0428. His want is visible in 0425.** **The floor was counted by reading and not by a string, and the sentence that puts him in each room is named here so a reader can check the count instead of taking it.** 0422, in person — *"A man came up those four flights at about the fourth hour with nothing in his hands and went along the boards and stopped where the boards end and stood there about the length of a sheet being squared, and did not buy anything, and went down the four flights again."* 0425, in person, and it is his chapter — *"He is thirty-eight and his name is Marek Kest, and he is not at a stage and is not going to arrive at one."* 0428, on the page and not in person, the cost of his absence named in the room he is not in — *"I have known for nine years what he is and I have said nothing to anybody about it for nine years and I chose it, and I chose it nine times a year on a rent day with my own two hands."* **`grep -l 'Kest'` returns 0425 and `grep -l 'thirty-eight'` returns 0425, the union is one, and the answer is three, and the two sweeps are wrong about two of the three chapters because in 0422 and 0428 he is in the room, or in the cost of the room, and is named in neither.** He holds nothing and was given nothing this batch: no office, no fund, no list, no leave, no stage, no certification, no commission, no warrant, no heading over his name, no second copy of anything, and no reading and no exercise of the thing he is able to do. **He is not thanked, nobody asked him anything, and his reason for not reaching a stage is referred to nowhere in these ten chapters and paid a second time zero times. And he is not a carrier: 0425 is the chapter in which a man he has never met says two true nine-word sentences about a piece of paper in a street outside his own door and is stopped twice, and Marek Kest stops him once by refusing to hear the same true thing twice in one week, and he is not thanked for that either, and nothing in that street made him a carrier and nothing in the batch pays the bill for being unfindable to him a second time.** His want is visible in 0425: the leaf, the struck name, the claim, and **the name and the name of the hand that struck it are printed in none of the ten.**

**Tamsin Rook, twenty-four, at the fourth counter, two years, in 0423 and 0424.** Nobody has put a question to her in this batch and nobody has put one to her in two years. **In 0423 she is the pair of hands a man can see across a counter and she says nothing, and she is the only person in that building who could have said something and is not going to. In 0424 she is the point of view and she has seen the turn happen and cannot say anything about it and is not going to, and she says one word about it in the whole chapter and the word is *no*, and nobody asks her for a word.** Her undertaking is live, unretired and unexercised, and **its date is not said out loud in any of the ten chapters and is not said out loud by this state record either, which is the standing and is honoured here.** **And in 0424 she is twenty minutes early again after having stopped in 0418, and Marn Ottery names the fact out loud and refuses to ask her about it, and the twenty minutes are not a decision and are not on anything, and she says in the room that she is not going to decide anything about it in that room. The decision about whether she trusts a colleague's judgment is not made in this chapter and is not made in this file.** She is not an organiser, not a clerk with a title, not a person given a post, not a person somebody has asked, and not a person who has been made a decision about.

**Marn Ottery, thirty-four, at the other end of the boards, eleven years, not a lawyer, in 0422, 0424 and 0430.** **In 0422 she tells a man who has wanted one thing for nine years that a supply in a room is a day's walk from about nine hundred rooms, and that the reason nobody can be told where the case is filled is that producing it is an office and an office has a name at the end of it — and that sentence is one the chapter says in her own words, because the phrase the card required is lifted from closed Chapter 0157 where it is Tamsin Rook's, and that is recorded in the batch summary and is not a theft and is a premise.** In 0424 she gives Tamsin Rook the half she has had to herself since, that there is no form in which to tell a man what a thing is for and there is not going to be one and that is not a wall, it is a room with the door open in it. **In 0430 she says out loud that she could have told the courier what was in the folded thing he put on her boards and did not, and that it would not have made him a party and she would have been within her rights, and that the reason is that there is no form anywhere in which to be shown what a thing is for and it has not changed in eleven years and is not going to change because she has found out that she is allowed.** In all three chapters she is the only person on that floor who can say what a piece of paper with nothing printed on it is for, and she said it out loud once, in the first week of the second month, and nobody thanked her, and she has said in terms that she is not going to be. **The box was not opened and did not move, the drawer was not opened, the case is still not the box, the fourth item is still the one none of the three of them can account for, and her want is unchanged: that the two women at that counter go on being the only two who know what is in that drawer, and that the arrangement that makes that true keeps standing, and it is still not on the page as a want and is not printed as one here.**

**The young man from the shipping floor, in 0423, 0424 and 0425, and the point of view of 0423, and the first time in this volume that a person who is not a clerk fills in a piece of paper.** Has packed for two years, carries about four sheets a week up four flights, and has read the top of about six of them by accident, and **in 0391 said a clerk who moves a box is a clerk who has made a statement about it and in 0403 was told he had made the same statement twice in front of two people and did not take it back.** **In 0423 he buys one at fourpence and fills it in by hand in about a minute and a half and folds it in three and buttons it inside his coat and goes down the four flights, and he says two sentences out loud into a room that gave him nothing to say them to and neither is addressed to a person and neither is picked up. He is not a villain, he has no plan, he is on no list in this matter, nobody asked him what it was for and he said nothing to anybody and nobody thanked him.** In 0425 he is right out loud twice in about nine words and is stopped twice, once by Marek Kest and once by the woman who keeps the ground floor, **and neither stop is unkind and neither stop takes the thing back out of the two people it had already been said to.** **He is not merged with the man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner and is not merged with the courier, and neither of those two is in this batch's chapter with him and no sentence of this batch can be read as a description of either of them.** What is written on the paper is printed nowhere and is not going to be.

**Halla Wray, fifty-one, a foreman in a box entered by a class, in 0421 and no other chapter of this batch.** **A man of about fifty-five in a shed tells her what a carrier is a second time without being asked for it, and she tells him out loud that the sentence has been in her head every working day since and she has not been able to do one single thing with it, and that a person who is told what a carrier is cannot un-know it, and that he does not get to have said it, and that being told what she costs is not a gift and is not a thing she came up here to give back.** She is not thanked for the four sentences she has said in that room over about a year, nobody asks her for a fifth, the form on the board goes back on it on the second of the two days next week, the form that put her there is not asked to be void by anybody, the two of seventeen at the back of that shed are not named in the chapter and are not spoken to, the lamp is still not lit until about the seventh hour, the second half of a sentence she began in a doorway has still not arrived, and **the four hundred yards up that lane are not walked on her account and not walked on his.**

**The man of about fifty-five with the chain, in 0421 and no other chapter of this batch, and the first time in this volume that something he said has come back at him.** **He is not one of the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph and never has been, and 0427 says so out loud about him by name while he is about nine miles off.** In 0412 he said the volume's sentence and the price of it and was not thanked. In 0421 a woman of fifty-one tells him the sentence has been in her head every working day since and that she cannot do one single thing with it, and he answers once — that a carrier's thing goes somewhere and hers is in a woman of fifty-one at the front of his own shed and is there for good — and **he is not thanked and is not going to be, and he cannot do one single thing about what he has been told, and that has never happened to him in that lane before.**

**The man who comes in on the second of the two days at the counter, in 0422, and the point of view, and the first chapter in eight volumes in which a person in this matter wants something out loud.** Not a lawyer and has not been one for about nine years, and the two facts are the same fact and have never been put side by side by anybody. **He says that he wants to know where the case is filled, and that he has wanted it every working day for about nine years, and that there is no form anywhere in this empire in which that could be put down — and he says it unasked for and not as a question and nobody asked him for it.** He is told the reason nobody can be told, refuses to treat it as an answer, does not ask for anything, does not say what he would have done with it, and is not thanked. **He is the only person in this matter who is examinable by nobody and wants something anyway, and he is not examinable and is not made examinable and is given no name and no age in this batch, and a man of about twenty-nine is not the man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner and the two are not merged.**

**The man of about sixty-one, the second hand since he was forty-two, in 0427 and no other chapter of this batch.** Cannot read a paragraph and has never once pretended to. **In 0427 the man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner tells him out loud what a filled-in sheet requires of a man at the foot of it, which is nothing at all — no name, no mark, no reading, no paragraph, no day, and nothing to fill in — and that nothing has ever required nothing of him in about nineteen years, and that the price of the first thing that does is that he is the most expensive object in this matter to touch and the cheapest to hand a piece of paper to. He answers once, to the pen pot, and it is the third thing he has ever said to that pot and neither of the other two: nineteen years, and the first thing that would have come up that stair wanting nothing off me did not come.** **His own figures are not used in this chapter: about nine hundred sheets a year and about nine of them a year that are not what the person above him wrote are printed in none of the ten, are not confirmed, are not denied and are not put in a book, he is asked nothing, and the nine miles is walked zero times, and the four men in that bay are still not asked one question about anything.** He is not thanked. **And the filled-in sheet does not arrive on his floor in this batch, and the reason is in a mouth and not in a record.**

**The man of about twenty-six who is paid by the corner, in 0427 and no other chapter of this batch, and this row still carries a disagreement that is not closed here.** **The batch prompt's cards of the last two batches called him the young woman and the man, `outline/volume-09.md` says *a man of about twenty-six paid by the corner* in two places and the state layer says it, and closed Chapter 0396 says *The young man who carries the sheets away* in its eleventh line and *The young woman took his hand off the sill. He was twenty-six.* in its thirty-first, and 0396 is closed and no phase may open it.** The page and the outline of record are with the two, so the chapter is written with a man, **and the disagreement inside 0396 is carried unrepaired on purpose, because a repair that picked would be a fourth figure where two are already unreconciled, which is the standing that carries item 83.** He is paid for a corner and not for a page and the basket goes down and does not come back up. **In 0427 he is not the young man from the shipping floor and is not merged with him and is in no room with him: he carries sheets up a stair for money, the young man packs and fills in a piece of paper at fourpence, and the chapter keeps them apart in every sentence and the next batch must too.**

**The man in the felt apron, in 0426 and no other chapter of this batch.** **He resists the woman at the lock by saying that a sheet with no heading printed over it is not a thing either of them has ever had to handle, that there is no rule on that passage about one, that the reason there is no rule is that no rule was ever needed, and that whoever is standing at this end when one comes down is the one who will have to decide what happens to it, and that is the person, and the person is her. And the architecture is not 0416's: he comes down to the lock and stands still, which he has not done at that end in forty years, and says the whole of it once, in one go, and it is about a person and not about a sheet.** She is refused nothing and asked nothing and thanks nobody. He is not thanked.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the lock end of about four hundred yards of cold flags, in 0426 and no other chapter of this batch.** Four years at that lock, never one of the three clerks, never sent for, thanked or handed a sheet. **She is told out loud that she is the person who would have to decide what happens to a sheet with no heading printed over it if one came down that passage, and she works the whole of it out standing at that lock — that a rule would be a form, a form is a heading over a ruled space, a heading is a place a thing is kept, and a thing that is kept can be shown, and a person standing at this end is not an office and is on no list — and she says not one word of it and nobody puts one thing to her about it.** Her figure is on no paper, has gone up, is not given away in any form in any sentence, is not handed on, and does not get a heading. **The four hundred yards is walked zero times, the rail at the other end is not hers and is empty and is not entered, and the woman of about forty with a scuttle is in no chapter of this batch, which is correct, and 0426 is a chapter at the other end of the same four hundred yards and does not put her in it.** **And the word *blank* is used zero times in 0426, because 0416 already stands at two uses at that end and the standing is at most once in this volume's whole run of chapters at that end, so the budget was spent by a prior batch and this one inherits a budget of zero.**

**The woman who keeps the ground floor of a rented house, in 0425 and 0428, and 0428 is the chapter the outline asks for.** About twenty years at the bottom of those stairs, never once above the second one, takes a rent, has never had his name and has never given him hers. **In 0425 she stops a stranger in her own street who says nine true words to her and tells him that she has a thing of her own in that house that nobody gave her and that she has carried about nine years, and that she is not going to be handed a second one off a stranger at the fifth hour, and that she has not said one word in that house in nine years that she did not have to say, and she does not go up.** In 0428 she is alone at the fifth hour and **says a true thing out loud to an empty room, once, and cannot take it back, and nobody thanks her and nobody sends for her and she does not go up those stairs, and the cost is that a true thing said out loud in a house is a thing the house can be shown, so the arrangement that made that floor the cheapest in the city has stopped being an arrangement. There is a tin behind the boiler with about nine years of his rent in it in coin and she does not move it, and the reason is that moving it is a sensible thing to do and a sensible thing done on purpose is a thing a person can be shown doing.** **She is not the clerk of about fifty-three, has never been inside that building, does not know what was said in it, and the two women are in different buildings in different chapters and no distance between them is stated or denied and they have never been in one building.** No romance, no moment in a doorway, and nothing between the two of them that is not the arrangement.

**The man of about thirty-four with the fair hand, in 0429 and no other chapter of this batch.** **He does not announce what he is going to say this time: he says it in the middle of a woman's week and she carries on, because if he makes a thing of it she has to make a thing of answering it and neither of them has a form for that.** He tells her, as a fact and not as a favour, that a book with four headings takes a thing in and gives it a column, that a sheet with nothing printed on it takes a thing in and gives it nothing back, and that a column with a heading over it is a machine that cannot say no and cannot be hurt by what comes back. He is findable and cannot be a carrier, **and the four words are not given, the figure in his head is not printed and not confirmed and not denied, he tells her nothing about his four days a season, he asks her for nothing, he knocks on nothing and she knocks on nothing, and the two of them start nothing.** He has a wife and a hall with a door two coats of paint; **the woman who keeps the day-book is the woman next door, has been eleven years, and is not the woman who stands at the foot of those four steps and never has been, and the two are not merged in this chapter and the separation is held in this row.**

**The woman of about thirty-five who keeps a day-book, in 0429 and no other chapter of this batch.** About nine years of a book with four printed headings over it — room, week, name, what was paid — and no fifth column ruled by anybody, and the fifth column is the only thing in that house she has wanted to do for about nine years. **She says out loud what she would use it for, which is a week when what was paid is not what was owed, and that she is not going to thank him for telling her what a column with a heading over it is, and that she is not going to take his second answer as an answer to a thing she did not ask him.** The book was not read, was not turned round, did not come off the shelf, and no fifth column was ruled. She is not thanked and does not thank him.

**The clerk of about twenty-nine, at the other end of two joined tables, in 0422, 0424 and 0430.** **In 0422 and 0424 she says nothing and keeps the promise she made about a second thing, and there is no form anywhere in this empire for a clerk to be recorded as the one who did not speak. In 0430 she says the finding of the movement out loud and accurately — that a filled-in sheet is a thing with no column, that a thing with no column cannot be filed, that a thing which cannot be filed stays in the room it was put in, and that this is not a failure of an arrangement and is the arrangement — and Marn Ottery does not treat it as an answer and does not thank her for it, and the statement lands on a third woman in the room who handed the man the paper a fortnight earlier and cannot speak about it.** She is not thanked. She is not merged with anybody and is not made a function.

**The courier, in 0430 and no other chapter of this batch.** A man from the yard with a satchel, and the cold in the leather where it sits against a man's hip, and **in 0430 he puts a folded thing on the boards the way he puts one there most days, says one thing about the cold, and is not told what is in the thing he has carried, and has never once asked and there is nowhere on that floor to tell him. And Marn Ottery says out loud, after he has gone down the stair, that she could have told him and did not, and that it would not have made him a party.** **He is not examinable, he is not made examinable, he is not thanked, nothing in this batch gives him a name or an age or a room, and he is not merged with the young man from the shipping floor and not merged with the man of about thirty-four with the fair hand and not merged with anybody else who is findable.**

**The woman of about forty with a scuttle, in no chapter of this batch, and that is correct.** **The claim that stood above, that she is in none of Volume 08's fifty files, was false against the page and is named here and deleted nowhere: she is in fourteen of the four hundred closed files, and Chapter 0362 is named for her and is set in the room with the rail from its first paragraph to its last.** What survives the correction is the instruction that was always the point, and this batch did not breach it: a Volume 09 writer may not give her a room, a trade, a week or a history the page has not given her, may not discover her where she was not, **and may not put her in a passage she was not written into — and Chapter 0426 is a chapter at the other end of the same four hundred yards and does not put her in it, and 0426 is the fourth batch running that has kept her out of a chapter at that end and that is correct.** She is not the woman of about twenty-six and the two are never in one room.

**Nell Kest, the two of seventeen, the girl of seventeen, the reader of seventeen, the woman of about twenty-two in her second season, the two women in their boxes, and Rennick Adley, thirty-eight, the Company's book-keeper: none of them is in a chapter of this batch except the two of seventeen, who are in 0421 at the back of a shed, unnamed and unspoken to, and the woman of about twenty-two who is not in any of the ten.** **Nell Kest is named in none of the ten and the four hundred and thirty miles is walked zero times, and no chapter of this batch put her in a room with a piece of paper or with a woman who carries one. A girl of seventeen and a reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked, unsent for, and not going to start.** **Rennick Adley is thirty-eight and so is the protagonist and the two men are in no chapter together and no sentence together and no sweep together, and the four hundred yards is not walked and the second half of the sentence in the doorway has not arrived.**

### The four debts, restated in full at Batch 0003, and this batch discharges none of them

**One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, which is four and not three.** Plus the review gate, which has opened and produced findings on all seven Batch 0005 phases the commit history records and has fallen back to the writing agent every time it has been asked and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, **and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either.** And the open items that are not plot, with the sentence bound among them, which are **eighty-nine by the high-water mark, eighty-seven entries measured in the rolling ledger before the eighth enforcement and three in it now, and the sixty-three this project has been quoting is twenty-four short, which is item 90, and this batch neither corrected that figure in place nor repeated it as true.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it. **The number of debts has not moved in eight volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero, and the third of those debts has a ninth instance now and the number of instances is not a measure of the debt: nobody but the hand that wrote these ten chapters has read them.**

# VOLUME 09 BATCH 0003 — CHARACTER STATE, REPAIR PASS AFTER `logs/batch-0003.review.log`

*Appended downward by the repair that took `logs/batch-0003.review.log`. **Nothing above this point is rewritten and no status about any person above is changed.** This is a repair and it is not a review, not a self-audit, not a second reading, not a close and not a second batch. It changed no plot and moved no person.*

**What the repair touched, and it is one mouth and one paragraph.** In 0429, Marek Kest said Chapter 0353's sentence about a machine — *a column with a heading over it is a machine, and a machine can be asked things all day long and does not mind the asking, because it cannot say no and it cannot be hurt by what comes back* — which is Marn Ottery's line, in her own rooms, in Volume 08, and he said it with a comma where she has a full stop. **He now says it his own way: those four have been taking whatever is put under them for about nine years and have never once objected, and the reason is not that they agree with you, it is that a thing under a heading has no way of saying it was never asked.** The substance is the same and the argument is the same and it is still his, and **he has still not been given any reason to have heard Marn Ottery say anything, and the repair did not supply one, and nothing in the page now says he has.**

**Nobody's status moved.** Marek Kest is thirty-eight, is named in 0425 and 0433's card and is not named in 0429, holds nothing, is not at a stage, is not going to arrive at one, and has still said one true thing in his working life and it is still unspent. The young man from the shipping floor is still not a villain, has no plan and is on no list, is still not the man of about twenty-six paid by the corner, and his own sentence about a no is now in his own words. The clerk of about twenty-four in 0423, who is Tamsin Rook and not the woman of about twenty-six, is still the only person in that building who could say something and is still not going to. **The woman of about twenty-six is at the lock end of the cold passage in 0426 and is in no other chapter of this movement, and she is not in 0423, and the first draft of this repair sentence merged the two and the merge is corrected here and not deleted.** The woman who keeps the ground floor in 0428 is still not the clerk of about fifty-three and has still not been in that building, and she has still not moved the tin. The clerk of about twenty-nine at the two joined tables has still said one thing in that room in about four years. The man of about fifty-five with the chain is in the shed in 0421 and is not in a bay with anybody. The man of about sixty-one is nine miles off a building off the old river road and is neither of the two women in their boxes.

**And two rooms that the repair made more distinct rather than less, because a room that is used four times in ten chapters is where a writer repeats themselves.** The counter at the top of four flights: 0422 opens on the boards and the six foot of bare deal between the case and the box, 0423 now opens on four people doing an ordinary morning and on the case being a thing four people have walked past about nine times a day for eleven years, 0424 opens on the first sound of a working day and keeps the case, the box, the wall board and the drawer, and 0430 now opens on the fourth item on the wall board and gets the case and the box and the drawer after it. **Same counter, same four items, same eleven years, same box, same drawer, four different mornings, and no two of the four open on the same twenty words any more.** The lane where a woman of about thirty-five keeps a day-book on a shelf behind a counter: 0384 is the same room and the repair made it the same room rather than a copy of it, described from the front of the book on the morning she does the week rather than from the shelf.
